# plan.md — Research Automation Platform: Master Plan

> **This is the single living plan for the workspace.** Every developer and every AI agent
> reads it before starting work and saves it after every status change (see §1 Rules).
> If it isn't recorded here, it didn't happen.

**Source of truth for scope:** [research-automation-spec.md](docs/research-automation-spec.md) (referred to as "the spec"; `§` numbers below point into it).
**Approach:** evolve the existing `research-main` FastAPI app (Postgres, SQLAlchemy, Alembic, DB worker). ARC stays a separate microservice for web search / crawl / PDF extraction only.
**Audience:** multi-user lab (roles, sharing, supervisor/co-author approvals are in M0).

**Repos covered**
- repo root (`research-main/`) — app root `backend/app/` (routers, `agent/`, `models.py`, `worker.py`, `web/`)
- `../AutoResearchClaw-main/AutoResearchClaw-main/` — ARC retrieval microservice (`researchclaw/server/retrieval_api.py`)

**Reference docs:** [ARC_INTEGRATION PLAN.md](docs/ARC_INTEGRATION%20PLAN.md) (design for M1.1) · [Researchee2e Plan.md](docs/Researchee2e%20Plan.md) (codebase analysis)

---

## 0. Legend

### Status
| Mark | Status | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `[ ]` | TODO | Not started, unclaimed |
| `[~]` | IN-PROGRESS | Claimed and being worked on — **must** have an owner |
| `[?]` | REVIEW | Work finished, awaiting verification/review by someone else |
| `[!]` | BLOCKED | Cannot proceed — **must** state the blocker |
| `[x]` | DONE | Verified complete (tests pass / acceptance check met) |
| `[-]` | DROPPED | Decided not to do — **must** state why |

### Priority (work in this order)
| Priority | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **P0** | Critical — broken, security risk, or blocks other work. Do first. |
| **P1** | High — required for the milestone to be considered done. |
| **P2** | Medium — should do this milestone; can slip one. |
| **P3** | Low — nice-to-have / backlog. |

### IDs
`M<milestone>.<task>.<subtask>` — e.g. `M1.6.2`. `X` = cross-cutting. IDs are **permanent**: never renumber or reuse; append new items with the next free number. Legacy IDs from the first version of this plan are shown as `(was 1.7)`.

### Task line format
```
- [~] **M1.6** Task title — `P1` — owner: @name — since: 2026-10-01 — deps: M0.4 — spec: §5.2.2
  - [?] **M1.6.1** Subtask title — `P1`
```
`spec:` is the spec section the task satisfies; `code:` (when present) is the existing place to extend.

---

## 1. Rules for multiple agents / developers

### 1.1 Before starting work
1. **Pull / re-read `plan.md` fresh.** Never act on a cached copy — another agent may have changed it.
2. **Pick the highest-priority unclaimed task** whose `deps` are all `[x]`. Order: P0 → P1 → P2 → P3, then lowest milestone, then lowest ID.
3. **Claim it first, then work.** Set `[~]`, `owner: @you`, `since: <date>` and save `plan.md` *before* touching any code. A claim is the lock.
4. **One task in progress per agent/developer.** Finish, hand off, or release before claiming another.
5. **Never take over a claimed task.** A claim older than **48 h** with no Work Log entry is stale: add a Work Log note ("releasing stale claim on X") and then you may re-claim it.

### 1.2 While working
6. **Autosave the plan:** update `plan.md` immediately after *every* status change — claiming, finishing a subtask, getting blocked, or finding new work. Do not batch updates to the end.
7. **Stay inside your task's file scope.** If you must edit files another `[~]` task owns, coordinate first (Work Log) or mark yourself `[!]` BLOCKED on that task.
8. **Discovered work goes into the plan, not into your diff.** Add a new `[ ]` task/subtask with a priority and continue. Do not silently expand scope.
9. **Blocked?** Set `[!]`, write `blocked: <reason / who or what you're waiting on>`, release ownership if you won't come back, and move to the next task.

### 1.3 Finishing
10. **Done means verified.** `[x]` only when the acceptance check passes (tests green, smoke test run, or reviewer sign-off). Otherwise use `[?]`.
11. **No self-review for P0/P1 code changes.** A different agent/developer moves `[?]` → `[x]`.
12. **The test baseline must not regress.** Run `pytest -q` from the repo root before marking anything done; update the "Test baseline" field if the count changes.
13. **Append to the Work Log** (§5) — one line per session: date, who, task IDs, outcome.

### 1.4 Editing this file
14. **Edit only your own lines** (your claimed task, your Work Log entry, new tasks you add). Don't reorder, reword, or re-prioritize others' tasks — propose changes in the Decisions log (§3).
15. **Priority/milestone changes and dropping tasks** need a Decisions-log entry with the reason.
16. **Merge conflicts in `plan.md`:** keep *both* sides' Work Log entries; for a task's status take the most advanced state only if its owner agrees; never delete someone else's claim.
17. **Never record secrets** (tokens, passwords, API keys, real `.env` values) in this file.

### 1.5 Repo safety (all contributors)
18. **Default-off:** new capabilities (retrieval, uploads, tools) ship disabled and must not change existing endpoint/test behavior when off.
19. **Schema changes** go through a new Alembic migration (`YYYYMMDD_NNNN_slug.py`) — never edit an applied migration.
20. **Don't commit** `*.db`, `*.db.bak`, `.venv*/`, `__pycache__/`, or `.env`.

### 1.6 Research-integrity rules (from the spec's principles)
21. **Gates can't be bypassed.** No agent or code path may approve a gate on a human's behalf, auto-approve on timeout, or proceed past a `blocked` state (spec §8).
22. **Fail loudly.** On LLM or retrieval failure, record the failure and stop the step. Never write canned/placeholder content and mark the step done (spec principle 2).
23. **Retrieved text is untrusted data.** Web/PDF/abstract text is fenced and labelled in prompts and never treated as instructions (see M1.2).
24. **Numbers come from code; citations come from verified records.** No generated statistic or reference is stored as verified without a computed source or a resolved record (spec §7.3–7.4).
25. **Every AI output records** `model_id`, `prompt_version`, inputs and actor (once M0.4/M0.8 land; new AI features must not ship without them).
26. **Don't import ARC pipeline code** (it fails open and auto-approves gates). Port ideas; call ARC only through its HTTP retrieval service.

### 1.7 Oversight rules (product-owner sign-off)
27. **Definition of done.** A milestone is done only when its spec §12 exit criterion and the related §13 acceptance criteria pass. Each `M*.exit` task names the criterion it proves.
28. **Milestone report.** Every milestone (and every demo) ends with a report in `milestone-reports/` using [TEMPLATE.md](milestone-reports/TEMPLATE.md): features vs [traceability.md](docs/traceability.md), test + gold-set results, known issues/deferrals, spec deviations.
29. **Non-negotiables at every demo.** Record pass / fail / not-built for N1–N5 (`docs/traceability.md` §3): citations click through and unverifiable ones are blocked; AI outputs show producer/model/date/approval; gates block; earlier-stage changes mark downstream stale; draft numbers link to analysis runs.
30. **Short cycles.** A working demo every 1–2 weeks, in milestone order (M0 → M3 first). No big reveal at the end.
31. **Traceability stays current.** Update `docs/traceability.md` whenever a task is added, finished or dropped. Spec lines with no task are gaps (add a task); tasks with no spec line go in its "no spec line" section with a reason. A deliberate spec change goes in the Decisions log **and** is edited into the spec.
32. **UI starts from a wireframe.** No screen in M2+ is built before its wireframe has been walked through by the scholar (X.14).

### 1.8 Local working rules (developers & agents on one machine)

**Claiming (works for any tool or person, not just Claude)**
- Every agent/IDE/human follows the same claim steps, whichever instruction file their tool reads (`CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`, Cursor/Copilot rules, or none). `AGENTS.md` is the canonical copy; if you can't see the rules, open `plan.md` §1 yourself.
- To claim, hand-edit your task line in `plan.md`: `[~]`, `owner: @name`, `since: YYYY-MM-DD`; save the file; then start work. No tooling needed.
- **Claims are only visible inside one working tree.** Developers on separate clones/machines can't see each other's claims until `plan.md` is shared (a commit/push the user approves). Before claiming, ask the user whether others are working elsewhere; if so, agree a task split in chat first and write it in the Work Log.

**Separate clones (Codex, another IDE, another person)**
- A claim only counts once the other clone can see it. With separate clones the claim must travel through git: a **claim-only commit** touching nothing but your `plan.md` task line, pushed to the shared base branch (`main`) *before* you code. This is a git action: do it only when the user approves it, and `git pull` (or `git fetch` + read `origin/main:plan.md`) before choosing so you see others' claims.
- If the user doesn't want claim commits, use a manual split instead: the user assigns task IDs per person/tool in chat, and each clone marks only its assigned tasks. Never pick a task outside your assignment.
- Prefer disjoint areas: assign by milestone or file scope so two clones never edit the same files. Overlapping files across clones is resolved by the user, not by whoever pushes first.
- Merge `plan.md` per rule 16 (keep both Work Log lines; never drop another clone's claim).

**Multiple developers on different branches**
- Each developer's `plan.md` lives on their own branch, so a claim on your branch is invisible to everyone else until it reaches `main`. **The source of truth for claims is `origin/main:plan.md`**, not your branch copy.
- One branch per task, named `<owner>/<task-id>-<slug>` (e.g. `revati/M2.3-screening-ui`), created from current `main`. Don't stack unrelated tasks on one branch.
- Before choosing: `git fetch`, then read `origin/main:plan.md` (`git show origin/main:plan.md`) and every open branch/PR title for active claims. Skip any task claimed there or whose files overlap an open branch.
- To claim: a **claim-only change** (your task line in `plan.md`, nothing else) must land on `main` first, via a tiny PR or direct push. Then branch from that `main`. Git actions need the user's approval each time (rule 41). If claims can't be pushed, the user assigns task IDs in chat instead and you work only on those.
- Keep `plan.md` edits on a task branch limited to your own lines (status, subtasks, Work Log) to keep merge conflicts trivial; resolve them per rule 16.
- Before opening a PR: fetch, check `main` didn't claim or finish the same task meanwhile, and confirm your files don't collide with another open branch. Finished status (`[?]`) goes in with the PR; `[x]` is set by a reviewer after merge.
- Branch protection: don't merge your own P0/P1 PR (rule 11); don't push directly to `main` except claim-only changes the user approved.

**Integrating into `main` (rules 47–56; git actions still need user approval, rule 41)**
47. **`main` is the only integration point.** No long-lived shared dev branch. Task branches are short-lived (aim ≤ 3 days) and cut from the latest `main`.
48. **Land via PR only.** One task branch → one PR to `main`. Title starts with the task ID; the description lists files touched, tests run, the `pytest -q` result and the baseline delta.
49. **Sync, then test.** Before the PR: fetch and merge `main` into your branch (**merge only — never rebase**, and never rewrite history on a pushed branch), then re-run `pytest -q` *after* the sync, not before.
50. **Merge gate.** Merge only if: full `pytest -q` green, baseline not regressed, a different person/agent approved (rule 11), `plan.md` conflicts resolved per rule 16, and `alembic heads` shows exactly one head.
51. **Merge order.** P0 first, then lowest milestone/ID; a task waits for its deps' PRs. When two PRs conflict, the first merged wins and the second syncs onto it. Nobody force-pushes `main`.
52. **Migrations are serialized.** Two branches must not both ship the same `NNNN`. The second to merge renumbers its migration, re-verifies up/down/up on a scratch DB, and re-checks the single head.
53. **`plan.md` after merge.** The merger updates the "Test baseline" field in the merge; the reviewer sets `[x]`. Every branch's Work Log lines are kept.
54. **Hotspot files** (`models.py`, `schemas.py`, `main.py`, `config.py`, `plan.md`, `docs/traceability.md`): make small, additive edits. The later PR resolves the conflict; it never drops the earlier side's change.
55. **After merge.** Delete the task branch (user-approved); others fetch `main` and re-check claims. A broken `main` is P0: revert first, fix second.
56. **Unmerged work is visible.** `git branch -r --no-merged origin/main` lists it. Branches idle > 7 days with no Work Log entry are reported to the user, never deleted by an agent.

**Choosing a task**
33. **Check before you choose.** Run `git status` and `git log -5 --oneline`, then re-read `plan.md`. List every `[~]` task and the files it owns; do not pick a task whose files overlap an active claim.
34. **No claim, no code.** If you can't claim a task (nothing eligible, all deps open), stop and report — never start "small" unplanned edits. Docs/rules-only edits still get a task line.
35. **Ambiguous or gated?** Tasks that need a scholar decision (Q-items in §4, gates, wireframes) are `[!]`, not guessable. Ask the user; don't assume the answer.

**Executing**
36. **Smallest change that meets the acceptance check.** Touch only files in your task's scope; tests mirror `app/<area>.py` as `test_<area>.py`.
37. **Verify in layers.** Run the task's focused tests while working; run full `pytest -q` from the repo root before `[?]`/`[x]`. Report failures with their output — never mark done on a red or unrun suite, and say plainly when the full suite was not run.
38. **Use `.venv` only** (never `.venv-retrieval`). Never run destructive commands against a real `.env` or non-scratch database; migrations are verified on a scratch SQLite DB.
39. **Stop and hand off cleanly.** At session end the plan, Work Log and your task status must match reality (`[~]` only if you'll return; otherwise `[?]`, `[!]` or release to `[ ]`).

**Git status & the working tree**
40. **The tree is shared and often dirty.** Uncommitted changes you didn't make belong to someone else's task. Never revert, stash, reset, checkout or delete them. Edit around them; if your task needs the same file, coordinate via the Work Log first (rule 7).
41. **Read-only git by default.** `status`, `diff`, `log`, `show`, `blame` are always fine. **Commit, push, branch, merge, rebase, stash, tag, reset and `--force` anything only when the user explicitly asks, per action.** Approval doesn't carry over to the next action.
42. **Commits are always manual, made by the developer.** Agents leave changes in the working tree and hand off a list of changed files plus a suggested commit message; they do not commit unless that developer explicitly asks in that session. **If you do commit:** stage explicit paths only (never `git add -A` / `.`), review `git diff --staged`, and include only files from your task. One task → one commit; message starts with the task ID (e.g. `M1.2.5: …`). Never `--no-verify` or amend others' commits.
43. **Never stage or commit** `*.db`, `*.db.bak`, `.venv*/`, `__pycache__/`, `.env`, secrets, or `.claude/` local settings (rule 20).
44. **Untracked ≠ junk.** New untracked files (new routers, migrations, tests) may be another task's unfinished work; don't delete or "tidy" them. Before creating a migration, list `backend/migrations/versions/` for the next free `NNNN`.
45. **Report the tree honestly.** In your handoff note which files *you* changed, run `git status --short` last, and call out anything unexpected rather than cleaning it up.
46. **Base-branch sync is the user's call.** Don't pull/merge/rebase `main` into your branch unprompted; if a conflict touches `plan.md`, follow rule 16.
57. **Step-by-step order (owner, 2026-10-10).** No new stage's screens while the current stage has open red items; verified extraction and evidence (M3.3, M3.4, M3.11, M3.14, M3.6) come before more screens or analysis; the screening screen (M2.3) is never built before the X.14.2 walkthrough; never weaken the citation block, gates, append-only audit, untrusted-text fencing, no-placeholder-on-failure, or numbers-only-from-code. Every claim of "works" says whether it was tested offline only, on SQLite only, or on a live API/model.

### 1.9 Reviewing other agents' work (owner request, 2026-10-14)

Anything built by one agent or person is reviewed by a different one. These rules say how to pick that review and what to record.

58. **Review before you build.** After re-reading `plan.md` (rule 1), list every `[?]` task. If any was built by someone else, claim one review before claiming new build work. Order: P0 → P1 → P2 → P3, then lowest milestone, then lowest ID. P0/P1 reviews are required by rule 11, so they come first. The only exception is a task the user assigned you by ID in chat.
59. **Never review your own work.** A task is not eligible if its `owner:` is you, if the Work Log shows you built any part of it, or if you wrote code for it in this session. A task owned by `@claude` can be reviewed by `@codex`, `@copilot` or a person, and the reverse. If every `[?]` task is yours, say so and ask the user for a named reviewer; do not review your own.
60. **Claim the review.** Leave the status at `[?]`. Add `reviewer: @you — review since: YYYY-MM-DD` to the task line and save `plan.md` before reading any code. One review at a time. A review claim older than 48 h with no Work Log entry is stale (rule 5).
61. **Re-verify; don't trust the author's evidence.** Read the task line's claim, the code and its tests. Run the focused tests, then the full `pytest -q` from the repo root. Check the change against rules 21–26. Where the task says a test guards a behaviour, repeat the mutation check (the test must fail without the change). Say whether each result is offline only, SQLite only, or on a live API/model/Postgres.
62. **Findings, not fixes.** Do not edit the author's code during a review. Record a defect as a finding on the task line, or as a new task (rule 8), and leave the status at `[?]`. Fix it in a later session only if the user asks.
63. **Outcomes.** Accept → `[x]`, but only on evidence you obtained in this environment. If part of the check cannot run here (a GitHub Actions run, a browser check, Postgres, a live model), leave `[?]` and state exactly what is missing. Tasks that need a named human (X.14.2, X.15, gate approvals, Q-items) cannot be accepted by an agent.
64. **Record the review.** On the task line: `reviewed YYYY-MM-DD by @you: result; tests run (counts); caveats`, and remove your `reviewer:` field. In the Work Log: one line with the task IDs, the outcome and the test counts. In the header table: update `Last updated`, `Updated by`, the awaiting-review part of `Current focus`, and `Test baseline` if the count changed.
65. **Nothing to review?** Say so in your handoff and continue with rule 2.

Work order: **M0 → M1 → M2 → M3** is the spec's MVP and is fully broken down. M4–M7 and X are task-level only; break them into subtasks when a milestone is about to start (add them under the existing task, next free ID).

Google Scholar gives no DOI, so it is **derived only from places that identify the paper itself**: the item URL when it is a doi.org/dx.doi.org link, or a publisher path that embeds it (`/doi/[abs|full|pdf|epdf|…]/10.…` — Wiley, T&F, SAGE, ACM; Springer `/article|chapter|book/10.…` and `/content/pdf/10.….pdf`; trailing page names like `/abstract`, `/fulltext.html` stripped); for PDFs also the PDF's own metadata (`doi` key, or `subject`/`keywords` holding exactly one distinct DOI — two → null). Never from body text, abstracts or query strings (the first DOI-shaped string there is often a cited reference, and research-main de-dupes ARC ingest by DOI, so a wrong DOI would drop a real source). research-main side unchanged: `arc_client._doi` already validates/normalises; checked end to end in one process (ARC `_serialize` → research-main `_normalize` → DOIs come through). Fix migration `20261003_0009` on Postgres: it calls `gate_code`/`gate_status` `.create(checkfirst=True)` and then `op.create_table`, which emits `CREATE TYPE` again → "type already exists", upgrade aborts. Offline `alembic upgrade 20261003_0008:20261003_0009 --sql` against a Postgres URL shows each `CREATE TYPE` twice. Fix: pass `postgresql.ENUM(..., create_type=False)` (or drop the explicit `.create()`); check M0.6.1's migration for the same pattern. Minor: the backfill's `bind.execute(...).fetchall()` breaks offline `--sql` generation. Found in review of M0.5.2 (review otherwise clean: model, policy, unique constraint, bootstrap and `ensure_gates` match the task; 5/5 tests pass; full suite 223 passed). M0.5.2 stays `[?]` until this lands — `P1` — deps: M0.5.2 — code: `migrations/versions/20261003_0009_gates.py` — needs review (P1): `app/stage_machine.py` + `POST /api/projects/{id}/stage/advance` (owner or co-author; body optional) and `GET /api/projects/{id}/stage` (everyone: current stage and the next stages, each with its gate, the gate's status and whether it is `ready`). **One step forward at a time in Appendix C order**; reaching a stage that has a gate needs that gate *approved by a person* — otherwise 409 with the gate, its status and who may approve it, and nothing changes (a rejected gate also blocks). Stages without a gate (retrieved, synthesized, data_collected, revised, …) need no approval. Skipping, going back, or repeating the current stage → 409 (going back is re-entry). After `submitted` the caller must choose `revision_loop` or `accepted`; `revision_loop` → `accepted`; `accepted` is final (resubmission = re-entry, then forward again). Passing the target stage makes a retried request safe (the repeat is refused, not applied twice). Audited as `project.advanced` with from/to, the gate and **who approved it**. It never touches gates or artifacts. The same function serves system producers: a producer (e.g. `agent:search`) can advance an ungated stage but can **never** satisfy a gate — it raises `GateNotApproved` and approves nothing (tested); a static test requires that only `stage_machine.py` and the re-entry code assign `project.stage`. Re-entry composes correctly: after going back, the voided gate must be approved again before moving on (tested end to end, plus a full walk idea → submitted that stops at all eleven gates). Task state changes race (stale worker can overwrite a newer attempt): `heartbeat`/`complete_task`/`fail_task`/`block_claimed_task` check ownership in Python (`_owned_running_task`: status running + attempt match) and then write by primary key, with no row lock and no condition in the UPDATE. Reproduced on scratch SQLite by interleaving in that gap: A claims attempt 1 → lease expires, reaper requeues → B claims attempt 2 → A's `complete_task` returns True and the task ends `completed` while B is running it (B's later complete is then refused). Same shape: `pause_task`/`block_task` vs a concurrent claim can mark a running task paused/blocked; worker `fail_task` vs the reaper can double-count. Fix: lock in `_owned_running_task` (`db.get(Task, id, with_for_update=True)`) and lock the row in pause/block/resume, or use conditional UPDATEs (`WHERE status='running' AND attempts=:attempt`) and check rowcount; add interleaving tests like M0.5.9's.   - [x] **M0.6.4** Idempotency keys so a retried task can't double-insert (e.g. ARC ingest) — `P1` — owner: @claude — since: 2026-10-03 — needs review (P1): **Tasks:** `tasks.idempotency_key` + unique index per project; `enqueue_task(..., idempotency_key=)` returns the existing task for a repeated key (no second row, no repeated audit event); the DB index is the backstop for a true race (the loser gets an `IntegrityError`, not a duplicate). `create_research_run` uses `research_run:<run_id>`. **ARC ingest:** `sources.ingest_key` (unique per project; NULL for hand-added sources) = `arc:<run_id>:<hash of url | doi | title+year>`, so a retried run skips what it already stored — including title-only papers that the URL/DOI check could never catch — and what it stored counts toward `MAX_ARC_SOURCES_PER_RUN`, so a retry can't exceed the cap; an unchanged retry writes no `sources.retrieved` event. API: `POST /api/projects` accepts an optional `discipline`; `GET /api/projects/{id}/profile` (everyone on the project) returns the discipline, the project's own overrides and the **effective** settings; `PUT /api/projects/{id}/profile` (**owner only**: it decides databases and reporting norms) replaces the choice and overrides, `discipline: null` clears it; each change is audited as `project.profile_changed` with before/after. A stored discipline whose profile later disappears does not make the project unreadable (GET profile → 409 with a clear message; PUT fixes it). Unknown disciplines are refused on input only, with the available ones listed. Role matrix extended. — reviewed 2026-10-05 by an independent reviewer session: claim matches code, named tests pass, no defect found

HTTP/transport errors are not retried here; an invalid schema or attempts < 1 fails before any call.   - [x] **M0.10.2** Object storage abstraction for PDFs/full text (local disk first; S3-compatible later) — `P2` — owner: @claude — since: 2026-10-07 — needs review (P2): new `app/object_storage.py`: `ObjectStore` protocol (`put`/`get`/`exists`/`stat` → `StoredObject(key, size, sha256)`), `LocalDiskStore`, `get_object_store(settings)`, `fulltext_key(project_id, source_id, ext)` → `projects/<p>/sources/<s>/fulltext.<ext>` (ext allow-list pdf/txt/html/xml/json) — the value meant for `Source.fulltext_path`. Keys are strict relative names (ASCII segments, no `..`, no absolute/drive/backslash, ≤16 segments, ≤512 chars) plus a resolved-path containment check (symlink escape refused). Objects are **write-once**: same bytes again = no-op, different bytes = `ObjectConflict` (M3.4 quotes must not change underneath them). Writes are atomic (temp file + `os.link`, so a concurrent writer is never overwritten); size cap `OBJECT_STORAGE_MAX_BYTES` (default 50 MiB). Settings `OBJECT_STORAGE_BACKEND` (only `local`; anything else raises — no silent fallback), `OBJECT_STORAGE_ROOT` (default `./data/objects`, git-ignored). Docker: `/data/objects` created and owned by `app` in the `Dockerfile`; `docker-compose.yml` mounts a shared `object_data` volume on web + worker. Found and fixed a Windows bug while testing: `Path.resolve()` sometimes returns a `\\?\` long-path while another writer is creating directories on the path, which made valid keys look like escapes (reproduced: 11 false rejections in 150 nested-key races before, 0 after). No callers yet (M2.7 will use it); no delete (permanent deletion needs a retention decision). `config.py`/`.env.example` edits are additive (other sessions had uncommitted edits there). `tests/test_object_storage.py` (32 passed, 1 skipped where symlinks aren't permitted). `pytest -q` → 1417 passed, 5 skipped. Settings: `OBJECT_STORAGE_BACKEND=s3` plus `OBJECT_STORAGE_S3_{ENDPOINT_URL,BUCKET,REGION,ACCESS_KEY_ID,SECRET_ACCESS_KEY,PATH_STYLE,CONDITIONAL_PUT}` (secret key is `SecretStr`, same convention as every other API key in `config.py`); `validate_production_settings` fails loudly (any environment, not just prod) if `s3` is selected without all four required fields — a test construction-checks both the all-missing and secret-only-missing cases. `get_object_store` now returns `S3Store` for `backend=s3`, `LocalDiskStore` for `local`, raises `ObjectStorageError` for anything else. No live S3/MinIO server reachable here: tested against `httpx.MockTransport`-style fakes (`_FakeS3Transport`) covering round-trip, not-found, no-op-on-same-bytes, conflict-on-different-bytes, over-cap refusal, non-bytes rejection, the conditional→fallback path, `conditional_put=False`, and a check that an always-500 backend's error message never contains the secret key. `tests/test_object_storage.py`; `pytest -q` → 2000 passed, 6 skipped. **Not verified against a real AWS/MinIO/R2 endpoint** — SigV4 implemented from the published algorithm, not checked against an official AWS test vector. — reviewed 2026-10-09 by an independent reviewer session: code matches the claim (`_sign_v4`'s canonical request/string-to-sign/4-round HMAC key derivation follow the published SigV4 algorithm exactly; `validate_production_settings` checks all four S3 fields before the `environment != "production"` early return, confirmed by reading `config.py:156-169`; `get_object_store` branches local/s3/error; no `boto3` in `requirements.txt`; `OBJECT_STORAGE_S3_*` documented in `.env.example`).   - [x] **M0.11.1** Test: create project → add co-author → events logged → gated task blocks until supervisor approves — `P1` — owner: @claude — since: 2026-10-03 — needs review (P1): `tests/test_m0_exit.py` — the spec's M0 exit ("create a project, share it, log events, run a gated task") as one narrative through the public API and the worker. An owner creates a project and invites a co-author, supervisor and reviewer; the co-author contributes, the reviewer can read but not write, an outsider sees nothing; a research run goes through the **durable queue** (202 queued, no model call in the request, the worker then completes it, with model and prompt version recorded); a task that needs gate G2 is created **blocked** and the worker won't touch it; a co-author and a reviewer can't approve G2 (403), a supervisor's rejection leaves it blocked, and only the supervisor's approval releases it, after which the worker runs it **exactly once**; the audit trail then contains the whole story in order with the right person against each step (including who approved), is readable by the team, exportable as CSV, and hidden from the outsider. A second test shows the gated task never runs however many times the worker polls or sweeps without an approval. **Mutation-checked, one break per pillar:** anyone-may-approve-G2 (roles), approval not releasing the task (gates), enqueue ignoring the gate (queue), and audit events not recorded (audit) each fail the exit check at the matching step; a harmless rewrite still passes. 2 tests. **This does not close M0:** the exit check passes, but its dependencies are built and still `[?]` awaiting review, and open M0 work remains (below). — reviewed 2026-10-05 by an independent reviewer session: claim matches code, named tests pass, no defect found

Every source excerpt now sits in its own block `<<<SOURCE [S#] id=…>>> … <<<END SOURCE [S#] id=…>>>` with Title/Status/Details/Excerpt, where **`id` is a random 16-hex value generated per request, after the source text exists** and never stored, so a source cannot forge the line that closes its block; the system prompt tells the model block contents are data only, never to follow or repeat instructions in them (even ones claiming to be the user/system/admin or appearing to close the block early), and to flag instruction-like text. The block layout lives in the prompt file (`source_block` template, validated at load) so it is versioned with the wording; v1 keeps the legacy layout. The question and saved project context are not fenced (the researcher's own input). `tests/test_prompt_fencing.py` (10 tests, parsing the prompt structurally: one block per source, shared id, no source text outside blocks, a forged closing line stays inside its block, ids differ per request, end-to-end request to the model); mutation-checked (executor pinned back to v1 fails 2, a predictable id fails 1). Fencing reduces risk; it does not replace human review. **Coordination:** edited only `_agent_prompts`, the prompt pin and an `import secrets` in `executor.py` via exact-match edits while @claude-reviewer holds X.21.10 there. — reviewed 2026-10-05 by an independent reviewer session: claim matches code, named tests pass, no defect found
  - [x] **M1.2.2** Strip/neutralise instruction-like patterns and markup; cap lengths per source — `P1` — owner: @claude — since: 2026-10-03 — coordination: small exact-match edits to `_source_snapshot` in `executor.py` (X.21.10 is @claude-reviewer's) — needs review (P1): new `app/untrusted_text.py::clean_untrusted(text, max_chars)` produces the **model-facing copy** only — the stored excerpt (immutable, hashed evidence) is never altered (a test pins this). It (1) removes content people can't see but models read: HTML comments, script/style/iframe/object/embed/template blocks, and invisible/control characters (zero-width, bidi overrides, Unicode *tag* characters used for hidden-text smuggling, private use); (2) neutralises prompt-imitating markup: fence lookalikes (`<<<`, `>>>` → spaced out) and chat-template tokens (`<|im_start|>`, `[INST]`, `<<SYS>>` → `[chat-markup removed]`); (3) collapses whitespace and caps length *after* cleaning (ends in `…`, hidden padding can't eat the cap); (4) **flags but never deletes** instruction-like phrases (`ignore_instructions`, `role_override`, `prompt_exfiltration`, `role_marker`) because deleting words would silently change what evidence says. Applied in `executor._source_snapshot` to title, URL, locator and excerpt (so the run snapshot shows exactly what the model saw, plus `flags` per source); new caps: title 300, URL 500, locator 255 (excerpt 4,000 and the 24,000 total already existed). A flagged source's status line in the prompt gains `NOTICE: hidden content or instruction-like text was found … quoted data only` (clean sources unchanged), which the v2 system prompt already tells the model to report. `tests/test_untrusted_text.py` (29 tests incl. tag-character smuggling, unterminated comments, idempotence, false positives kept verbatim); mutation-checked (no invisible-char stripping fails 5, no fence neutralisation fails 2). **Limits, stated plainly:** regexes can't stop a determined injection — this lowers the odds and makes attempts visible; known false positives are flagged, not removed; removing zero-width joiners also splits some emoji sequences; abstracts/ARC `to_context_string` consumers are still M1.2.4; the real-model adversarial suite is still M1.2.3. **Coordination:** `executor.py` edits were limited to `_source_snapshot`, `_source_status` (new), three cap constants and one import. — reviewed 2026-10-05 by an independent reviewer session: claim matches code, named tests pass, no defect found

It proves four things. **(1) Request containment** — a reusable fixture library (`tests/adversarial_fixtures.py`, 21 hostile texts across override, role hijack, prompt exfiltration, delimiter forgery, chat markup, hidden text, evasion, structure-break, tool abuse, data requests, markdown exfil, padding; add a fixture and every test picks it up) is run through the whole pipeline both as a manual source and as ARC-retrieved text: the request keeps exactly one system + one user message, the system text is untouched, the hostile text sits only inside its single fenced block (canary strings never appear outside), hidden characters and prompt-imitating markup are gone, expected flags are reported, stored evidence is byte-identical, and the padding attack is cut off by the cap. **(2) Response shape** — a hostile run's API response has exactly the same keys, status and provenance as a benign run's. **(3) Answer-side leak guard (new code)** — `app/answer_guard.py::check_answer`, called by the executor: if the model's answer contains the per-request block id, fence markers, or a whole sentence of the system prompt, the run is **failed loudly and the answer is not stored** (`research_run.answer_rejected` audit event with model_id and prompt_version, but not the leaked text). Tested with a simulated *compromised* model that obeys the attack (4 leak shapes); ordinary answers, even ones that quote hostile text, pass; matching ignores case and spacing. It catches the common exfiltration shape, not a model that paraphrases. **(4) Isolation** — another project's title/sources/context never enter a request. Plus a static test that the front end never renders output as HTML (no `innerHTML`/`eval`/…). `tests/test_adversarial.py` (77 cases). Mutation-checked: leak guard off fails its 4 tests; removing the fence fails 46; not stripping hidden characters fails exactly the 3 hidden-text fixtures on both paths. **Known gaps kept visible as fixtures** (`known_gap=True`, asserted *unflagged* so the suite tells us when that changes): non-English injections (French) and homoglyph evasion (Cyrillic о) — only the fence protects the prompt there → new M1.2.5. **Coordination:** one more exact-match edit at the LLM call site in `executor.py` (X.21.10 is @claude-reviewer's). — reviewed 2026-10-05 by an independent reviewer session: claim matches code, named tests pass, no defect found

  - [x] **M1.3.2** Shared `httpx` base: retries with backoff + jitter honouring `Retry-After`, per-connector rate gate (instance state, not process-global), circuit breaker, timeouts — `P1` — reference only: ARC `literature/semantic_scholar.py`, `openalex_client.py` — owner: @claude — since: 2026-10-04 — needs review (P1): `app/connectors/http.py`: `ConnectorHttpClient(name, base_url, HttpPolicy, ...)` with `get_json(path, params, headers)`; one instance per connector, all state on the instance. **Retries:** 429/500/502/503/504 and transport errors, exponential backoff capped at `backoff_max`, full jitter; `Retry-After` (seconds or HTTP-date) honoured when longer than the backoff, but if it exceeds `max_retry_after_seconds` (60) we stop instead of sleeping. **Fails loudly:** exhausted retries → `ConnectorError` (message has connector name + status only — no URL, query or key); 404 → `NotFoundError` (so `get_by_id` can return None), other 4xx → immediate `ConnectorError`; non-JSON reply → `ConnectorError`; redirects not followed. **Rate gate:** `min_interval_seconds` spacing, per instance. **Circuit breaker:** opens after `breaker_threshold` (5) consecutive calls that exhausted retries; while open calls fail at once with `CircuitOpenError` (no network); after the cooldown exactly one trial call is allowed (success closes, failure reopens). 404s and bad requests don't count as outages. Clock/sleep/rng are injectable so tests don't wait. Defaults are my choice (timeout 20 s, 3 retries) — tune per connector. Sync only (matches the sync worker). Caching is M1.3.3; no connector uses this yet. `tests/test_connector_http.py` (21); mutation-checked (8 breaks: Retry-After ignored, 404 not special, redirects followed, breaker never opens, bad requests counted as outages, no rate gate, long Retry-After slept, multiple half-open trials → each fails ≥1; two breaks first survived, which exposed two test gaps that I closed). `pytest -q` → 656 passed. — reviewed 2026-10-05 by an independent reviewer session: claim matches code, named tests pass, no defect found

When > 0 an identical request (connector + method + path + params + body, order-insensitive) inside the TTL is answered from memory **before** the rate gate, breaker and network; only successful JSON is stored (never errors/404); returned data is a deep copy; bounded LRU (256); per-client, in-process, not shared between workers; headers are not part of the key. `cache_hits`/`cache_misses`/`last_from_cache` expose it; `get_json(..., fresh=True)` bypasses and refreshes. Key includes the connector name; a connector-version component isn't needed because the cache is per-process and dies with a deploy. `tests/test_connector_cache.py` (11). `pytest -q` → 1455 passed, 5 skipped. **Not done:** the search runner doesn't pass `fresh=True` or record `from_cache` in the search log — see M1.3.7; leave the TTL at 0 for real searches until then. — reviewed 2026-10-08 by an independent reviewer session: claim matches code (cache read before gate/breaker/network, successful replies only, deep copies, bounded LRU, sorted-key hashing); tests pass (14 with M1.3.7). Observation: `build_connector` makes a new client per task, so cached answers are only reused within one task — fine for the claim, worth knowing before relying on the cache to save rate limit.

  - [x] **M1.5.1** Concept blocks + synonyms → Boolean string (AND across, OR within) — `P1` — owner: @claude — since: 2026-10-04 — needs review (P1): new pure module `app/search_query.py` (no network/DB; deterministic). `ConceptBlock(label, terms)` = one concept with synonyms (OR-ed); `BooleanQuery(blocks)` = blocks AND-ed; `render(query)` gives the Appendix A shape, e.g. `("construct A" OR "synonym A1") AND ("construct B")`. **Terms can't change the query's structure, and are refused rather than silently altered:** quotes, parentheses, backslashes, control/invisible characters (incl. zero-width) and bare operator words (AND/OR/NOT/NEAR/ADJ) are rejected with a clear message (operator words *inside* a phrase are fine). Terms are trimmed, whitespace-collapsed, NFC-normalised; case-insensitive duplicates within a block are dropped (first kept); non-ASCII text survives. Every term is rendered as a quoted phrase **except** a single-word truncation like `employ*` (≥2 letters before a trailing `*`), which stays unquoted because most databases ignore wildcards inside quotes. Limits: 10 blocks, 50 terms/block, 100 chars/term, rendered query ≤ 2,000 chars (the connectors' ceiling) — a too-long query is refused when defined, not at search time. `label` is for the scholar's reference and is never rendered. Frozen models, unknown fields refused. **Out of scope here (other tasks):** per-database syntax (M1.5.2 — the generic string uses `AND`/`OR` and double quotes, which not every API accepts as is), NOT/exclusion terms (Appendix A has none; add when a criterion needs it), LLM synonym suggestions (M1.5.3), filters (they travel in `SearchRequest.filters`), storing/versioning a query (M1.7.1). No API route or UI yet. `tests/test_search_query.py` (32); mutation-checked (forbidden characters, operator check, dedupe, quoting, OR→AND, control-character check, length check at definition, NFC→NFD → each fails ≥1; the first forbidden-characters run was a sed that didn't apply and was redone). `pytest -q` → 846 passed. — reviewed 2026-10-05 by an independent reviewer session: claim matches code, named tests pass, no defect found
  - [x] **M1.5.2** Per-database syntax adapters (OpenAlex, S2, Crossref, arXiv, PubMed) — `P1` — owner: @claude — since: 2026-10-05 — needs review (P1): new pure module `app/search_query_adapters.py`: `adapt(BooleanQuery, database) -> AdaptedQuery(database, text, exact, caveats)` for `openalex`, `semantic_scholar`, `crossref`, `arxiv`, `pubmed`. **Honesty over convenience:** `exact` is True only when the database will apply the blocks' AND/OR logic as written; `caveats` are plain-language reasons it might not, to be kept with the search record (M1.7). **OpenAlex** — the generic quoted AND/OR string. **arXiv** — `all:"phrase"` terms with AND/OR/parentheses. **PubMed** — `"phrase"[tiab]` (title/abstract) so automatic term mapping can't widen the search; truncation renders as `employ*[tiab]` (unquoted — quotes switch truncation off). **Semantic Scholar and Crossref have no Boolean operators in the endpoints our connectors use** (`/paper/search`, `query=`), so they get a flat, de-duplicated plain-text query with `exact=False` and a caveat that results are *ranked, not filtered,* by the blocks — never presented as a faithful Boolean search. **Wildcards:** where support isn't clearly documented (OpenAlex, arXiv, Semantic Scholar) a query that actually uses `*` is marked inexact with a caveat; the same query without wildcards stays exact. Unknown database → `QueryError` listing the supported ones; a result longer than the connectors' 2,000-char ceiling (e.g. PubMed tags pushing it over) is refused, never truncated; hostile terms can't reach an adapter because `ConceptBlock` already refuses them. **Assumptions not yet checked against the live services** (stated in the module docstring): that OpenAlex `search=` honours uppercase AND/OR with quotes/parentheses and that arXiv and PubMed accept the forms above — needs a live smoke test with the connectors. Filters stay in `SearchRequest.filters`. No API route/UI; nothing wires adapters to connectors yet (the search runner, M1.7/M1.8). **Possible later improvement (new task M1.5.5, P3):** S2's `/paper/search/bulk` endpoint supports Boolean syntax (`+`, `|`, `-`) and would make S2 exact. `tests/test_search_query_adapters.py` (16); mutation-checked (PubMed tag dropped → 3 fail; arXiv prefix dropped, S2/Crossref reported exact, OpenAlex wildcard flag removed, length check off, wildcard star kept, dedupe off, unknown-database check off → each fails ≥1). `pytest -q` → 862 passed. — reviewed 2026-10-05 by an independent reviewer session: claim matches code, named tests pass, no defect found

Migration `20261005_0020` (additive; up/down/up and `alembic check` clean on scratch SQLite, single head; Postgres not run). **API** (`routers/criteria.py`, `app/criteria.py`): `GET /api/projects/{id}/criteria` (every member role) returns framework, its elements (for building the form), `locked`, `problems` (advice: no framework / no inclusion / no exclusion / framework elements with no criterion yet) and the criteria; `PUT` (owner or co-author; supervisors and reviewers read only) **replaces** framework + criteria in one transaction. Validation: kind, 3–1000 chars (whitespace normalised), ≤50 criteria, an element must belong to the chosen framework, codes unique and prefix-matched to kind, the same criterion not listed twice, unknown fields refused; any refusal changes nothing and writes no audit event. **Codes are stable:** a kept code keeps its number; new ones continue after the project's highest existing code, so a dropped `I2` is never handed to a different criterion (decisions that cite it would silently change meaning). `created_by` of a kept criterion is preserved. **G2 lock (rule 21):** once a person has approved gate G2 the criteria can't be changed (409 naming G2) — an approval for one set can't cover another; they unlock only when G2 is pending again (re-entry), and a rejected/pending G2 doesn't lock. Audited as `criteria.updated` with framework and the codes before/after — **not** the text. Role matrix extended. **Choices to confirm:** WRITE roles (owner + co-author) edit, approval stays with G2 approvers; `locked` keys off G2 only; PICO/PICOC/SPIDER element lists follow the usual definitions (SPIDER = sample, phenomenon of interest, design, evaluation, research type). **Not here:** screening decisions citing the codes (M2.1), criteria in AI screening prompts (M2.2 — text must be fenced then), a change-role/clone-from-template feature, UI. `tests/test_criteria.py` (27) + 2 role-matrix rows; mutation-checked (lock off, lock on any status, code reuse, `created_by` not preserved, element check off, duplicate check off, wrong role set, audit leaking text → each fails ≥1). `pytest -q` → 903 passed. — reviewed 2026-10-05 by an independent reviewer session: claim matches code, named tests pass, no defect found

  - [x] **M1.6.1** DOI normalisation (strip `https://doi.org/`, case-fold) then normalised title + year + first author — `P1` — owner: @claude — since: 2026-10-05 — needs review (P1): new pure module `app/dedupe.py` (no DB; works on anything with `doi`/`title`/`year`/`authors` — `PaperRecord`, `Source`, `DedupeRecord`). **Rules, cautious on purpose** (a wrong merge hides a paper; a missed duplicate costs a person a second look): (1) same normalised DOI → duplicate (`doi_key` reuses `normalize_doi`; a malformed DOI is ignored, not fatal); (2) else same **work key** = normalised title + year + first author's family name; a missing year/author only matches another missing one, and a title with too little content (< 15 "weight", so "Introduction" or "Women and work") gets no key; (3) **two different DOIs are never merged** even with an identical work key (preprint vs published, book vs review — a person decides). `match_kind(a, b)` → `"doi"` / `"title_year_author"` / None. `group_duplicates(records)` clusters input indices in input order, first record = anchor; a record joins the first earlier group it matches that doesn't already hold a different DOI, so a chain A~B~C can't pull conflicting DOIs together (order-independent, tested both ways). First author: "Lovelace, Ada" = "Ada Lovelace" = "A. Lovelace"; the key is the last word of the family name so "Garcia Lopez, M." = "Maria Garcia Lopez" and "Ludwig van Beethoven" = "Beethoven, Ludwig van". **Not here:** merging records / keeping all source ids and best abstract / logging decisions (M1.6.3), persisting groups, wiring into ingest or the search runner, fuzzy (edit-distance) matching, preprint↔published linking. `tests/test_dedupe.py` (42); mutation-checked (14 breaks: ASCII-only filter, Latin-only accent folding off/on-everywhere, HTML not stripped, NFKC/casefold off, different DOIs merged, DOI-conflict guard off, min-title off, CJK weight off, family-name rule, year ignored → each fails ≥1; one survivor — a particle-stripping branch made redundant by the last-word rule — was deleted as dead code). `pytest -q` → 945 passed. — reviewed 2026-10-05 by an independent reviewer session: claim matches code, named tests pass, no defect found
  - [x] **M1.6.2** Unicode-safe title normalisation (don't drop non-ASCII letters) — `P1` — owner: @claude — since: 2026-10-05 — built together with M1.6.1 (same module; ASCII-only normalisation would have to be written and then undone) — needs review (P1): see M1.6.1 — `normalise_title` is Unicode-safe: letters/digits of every script kept (Devanagari incl. vowel signs, Chinese, Cyrillic, Arabic — tested; unrelated non-Latin titles don't collide), NFKC (ligatures, fullwidth, composed/decomposed unify) + casefold, HTML tags/entities removed, punctuation → space, combining accents dropped **only on Latin letters** ("Café" = "Cafe"; Indic marks kept because they change the word); dense scripts (CJK/Kana/Hangul) count double toward the minimum-title rule so a 10-character Chinese title still identifies a work. — reviewed 2026-10-05 by an independent reviewer session: claim matches code, named tests pass, no defect found
  - [x] **M1.6.3** Merge records (keep all source ids and best abstract) instead of replacing; log merge decisions — `P1` — owner: @claude — since: 2026-10-05 — scope: the pure merge + decision log; applying it to stored `Source` rows is split out as M1.6.4 — needs review (P1): new pure module `app/merge.py`: `merge_records(group)` (the group from `dedupe.group_duplicates`, anchor first) → `MergeResult(record, members, matched_by, decisions)`; no DB access. **Nothing is dropped:** every member's `connector`/`external_id` and the ids it carried end up in `source_ids` (anchor stays the primary id); if two members give *different* ids under one key the anchor's wins and the other is written to the decision log (`id_conflict`) so it can still be found. **DOI:** first present (normalised); members with different DOIs raise `MergeError` (same rule as `match_kind`). **Abstract:** the longest, ties to the earlier record; **authors:** longest list; **title/year/venue/url/oa_url:** first present (anchor decides unless it has none). **Flags:** union, then re-derived — `no_abstract` is dropped once any member supplied an abstract (added if none did), `invalid_doi` is dropped once a valid DOI is known. **Decision log:** every field where members disagreed or a non-anchor supplied the value gets a `MergeDecision` (field, who was chosen, reason `first_present`/`longest`/`id_conflict`, who was passed over, with long text described by length); agreement logs nothing. `matched_by` says how each member joined (`anchor`/`doi`/`title_year_author`/`group` = joined by chain). `audit_payload()` gives ids and short descriptions only — no titles or abstracts — ready for `audit.record`. Deterministic; inputs are not mutated. **Not here (new M1.6.4, P1):** applying a merge to stored `Source` rows (keeping excerpts/verification, writing the `source.merged` audit event) — it needs the search runner's ingest path (M1.7/M1.8); the title/author choice rules are a simple first cut (anchor/longest) and may need refining against real data. `tests/test_merge.py` (16); mutation-checked (10 breaks: ties to later record, DOI conflict allowed, id overwritten, `no_abstract` kept, not re-added, member ids not added, authors from anchor only, `matched_by` always group, abstract text copied into the log, decisions never logged → each fails ≥1). `pytest -q` → 961 passed. — reviewed 2026-10-05 by an independent reviewer session: claim matches code, named tests pass, no defect found

Kept source = `keep`, else the verified one, else the oldest. Uses the pure `merge.merge_records` rules; the sources must form **one duplicate group** and never carry two different DOIs. **Nothing is deleted:** merged rows get `sources.merged_into = kept.id` (new column, migration `20261005_0023`, up/down/up + `alembic check` clean on scratch SQLite, single head; Postgres not run) and disappear from the source list, the verify route and prompts, but stay so old run snapshots, audit ids and `ingest_key`s (which stop a retried retrieval re-adding the paper) still resolve. Excerpts move to the kept source with content/hash untouched. **Verification is never promoted:** the kept source stays verified only if it was verified *and* every field value taken from another source came from a verified one; otherwise the flag is cleared and the audit event says so (`verification_cleared`); an unverified kept source is never promoted by a verified duplicate. `source.merged` audit event: kept/merged ids, how each matched, field decisions — no titles/abstracts. `source_ids` on the kept row = union of all members' ids (internal bookkeeping keys removed). New: `POST /api/projects/{id}/searches` `{database, blocks[{label,terms}], filters, max_results}` (owner/co-author; 202 `{task_id, search_id, version, status, blocked_by_gate}`; rejects a non-searchable/not-enabled database (400) and an invalid or un-adaptable query (422) **before** anything is queued), `POST .../searches/{search_id}/rerun` (next version with the same string/filters; 404 unknown, 409 if that search already has a run waiting/in progress; blocked again if G2 was voided by re-entry), `GET .../searches` (every role: searches with versions/counts/exactness plus pending tasks). `app/connectors/factory.py` builds OpenAlex/Crossref/Semantic Scholar from settings; `handle_search_run` in `task_handlers.py` runs `run_search`/`rerun_search` with the requester as actor/`created_by` (not the approver), is **idempotent** (a retry after a crash that already saved the run does nothing), commits the `search.failed` audit event then lets the queue retry with backoff (ends `failed` after max attempts, saving no run), and turns bad input into a permanent failure. `search_runner` takes optional `search_id`/`version` so ids/versions handed out at queue time hold; caps moved to `app/search_limits.py` so the API never imports the runner — a static test pins that only `task_handlers.py` uses `search_runner` and that it requires G2 (this replaces M1.7.2's "not wired anywhere" test). Lookup: DOI via `get_by_id` where a connector supports it (`NotSupportedError`/`ValueError` = skipped, not an error — OpenAlex's id lookup rejects DOIs), then title search (5 hits) on every connector; one service confirming is enough even if another failed or missed. Failures are collected in `errors`, never raised or turned into a verdict. **Strictness (choices to confirm):** year off by one is a mismatch (a person can still verify by hand); first author's family name must match and ≥ half of the reference's authors must appear in the record; titles match after the same Unicode-safe normalisation as de-dup, with a similarity ratio ≥ 0.92; a `retracted` flag is reported in `flags` alongside `verified`. `summary()` gives ids/verdict/reasons/errors-by-service only (no titles, authors or error text) for audit payloads. **Does not set `metadata_verified` or touch the DB** (a static test pins it) — storing the result and the only automated path to `metadata_verified` are M1.10.2; blocking unverified references is M1.10.3. **Limits:** Crossref/S2/OpenAlex only (no DataCite or arXiv connector yet, so DOIs registered elsewhere may come back `not_found`/`mismatch`-free); no live API run; no author-initial disambiguation beyond the family-name key; the fixture set here is small — M1.10.4 (known-good/fabricated/mismatched corpus) stays open. `tests/test_citation_verifier.py` (18); mutation-checked (10 breaks: year, first author, authors, DOI, title ignored; miss reported as mismatch; errors reported as not_found; unrelated hits counted as mismatch; incomplete references checked; ValueError not skipped → each fails ≥1). `pytest -q` → 1094 passed. — reviewed 2026-10-05 by an independent reviewer session: claim matches code, named tests pass, no defect found

- [x] **M1.12** Saved-query alerts — `P3` — deps: M1.7.2, M0.6 — spec: §5.2.4 — owner: @arena — since: 2026-10-10 — closed 2026-10-10 at the user's request ("start working on M12 … finish the task"; P3, full suite green): no separate reviewer needed for this P3 item.  "Alert subscriptions: re-run saved queries on a schedule and surface new papers." Design (recorded 2026-10-10): a subscription (`SearchAlert`, one per saved search) re-runs the stored query as its next version (M1.7.2 `rerun_search`) on an interval of the person's choosing (1 h – 30 d, default 1 d); a re-run is bulk retrieval, so its `query_alert` task **requires G2**, like M1.8.1. "New" = the new version's records minus the union of **every earlier version** of that search (a paper that left and came back is not re-surfaced). Surfacing = the new version row + the alert's last-run state + audit (ids/DOIs, capped at 50) — alert results are not saved as `Source` rows (M1.7.2/M1.6.4 line); no email/calendar (spec §11 notification services are out of scope). Scheduling = the worker loop sweeps due alerts and enqueues one task per due slot, idempotent per slot. A retried run after a crash is a no-op (`last_run_at >= enqueued_at`).

The criteria (with their codes) and ONE record are sent; the title/abstract go through `clean_untrusted` and a per-request-nonce fence (`<<<RECORD [R1] id=…>>>`), injection flags are audited. Reply must be JSON `{decision include|exclude|maybe, reason_code, confidence 0–1, rationale}` via `complete_json` (invalid after N tries fails that record, never repaired). A reason code that isn't one of the project's criteria or doesn't match the decision (exclude needs an `E` code) is **rejected, not corrected**; a rationale that repeats the nonce, fence markers or system-prompt sentences is rejected. Queued as task `screening_prescreen` (**needs G2**, so it waits until a person approves the criteria): `POST /api/projects/{id}/screening/prescreen` (owner/co-author; 202; asking again returns the open task). One record at a time, committed as it goes; records a person decided or the AI already saw are skipped, so a re-run carries on. A failed record is audited (`screening.ai_failed`) and left for a person; the task then ends failed with the count. No LLM configured → task fails at once. `GET …/queue` now shows each suggestion's rationale. Setting `PRESCREEN_MIN_CONFIDENCE` (default 0.6). **Not checked against a real model** (tests use a scripted reply through the real schema validation).
    - Review 2026-10-10 (independent reviewer session): claim matches code — `clean_untrusted` + per-request nonce RECORD fence, `complete_json` raises `LLMSchemaError` with no silent repair after N attempts, `check_reason()` rejects mismatched/unknown codes without correcting them, a rationale that leaks the nonce/fence/system text is rejected (combines `answer_guard.check_answer` with an explicit `<<<` check, needed because `answer_guard`'s fence regex only matches `SOURCE` fences, not this prompt's `RECORD` fences — a correct compensation, not a gap), task registered `requires_gate=GateCode.G2`, checksum pinned in `test_prompts.py`. `test_prescreen.py` → 18 passed. No defect.

When on, `app/prescreen.py::run_prescreen` samples every `1/screening_ai_autoexclude_sample_rate`-th (default 10%) eligible exclude (confidence ≥ threshold) into a `pending` `AutoExcludeAudit` row; unsampled eligible excludes are "trusted" and count immediately, sampled ones only once a person confirms (`POST .../auto-exclude/audits/{id}/review`, `WRITE_ROLES`, one-shot: the review is a conditional `UPDATE ... WHERE status='pending'` whose rowcount must be 1, else 409, so two concurrent reviews cannot both succeed; the `before_update` ORM guard `_guard_auto_exclude_audit_single_review` refuses a second review on the ORM flush path; the DB CHECK does not forbid a second review, it only requires the review fields to be set together). `app/screening.py::effective_ai_exclusion` is the single read-side function (used by `GET .../queue` counts/waiting list and `app/thematic_clusters.py::clusterable_sources`) so a human decision always overrides it and it's off automatically whenever `dual_screen_enabled` is on (two independent *human* reviewers and an unreviewed AI auto-exclude don't mix). So the classifier is trained on A's decision about the very records it then ranks for B: A's includes go to the top of B's list and A's excludes to the bottom. That reveals A's answer, which the blinding in M2.4.1 (`visible_rows`, AI suggestion withheld) exists to hide. The 2026-10-12 re-review's "ranking only, non-blocking" note understated this. Fix to re-review: under dual-screen, train only on settled (`agreed`) decisions or on the viewer's own decisions, never on another reviewer's rows for records still in the viewer's queue; add a regression test that fails today. (2) **Claim text:** the migration is `20261010_0043_active_learning.py` (revises `0042`), not `20261010_0042`. Reproduced with a throwaway test (since deleted): one AI exclude E1 at 0.95 with auto-exclude on → queue `ai_auto_excluded: 1, unscreened: 1`; PRISMA `excluded: 0, unscreened: 2`. The PRISMA excluded count and `exclusion_reasons_missing` therefore omit policy-excluded records, and the flow disagrees with the screening queue. Fix: count `effective_ai_exclusion` rows as excluded (and check their reason code) in `_screening_flow`, with a behavioural test. Observation for owner: G3 approval is not blocked by unscreened records (EC-4.3 is Recommended, `exit_conditions.py:291`), so the included set can be approved while records are unscreened; decide whether that should be Required.

  - [?] **X.37** — owner: @claude — since: 2026-10-07 — reopened/fixed 2026-10-09: this was the CI failure reported as "test suite failing on GitHub" (`test_arc_fencing.py`, 4 of 6 tests, reproduced on a clean checkout of `d5c65d0`, independent of any local uncommitted work). The `_begin_before_savepoint` hook already in `app/database.py` (issuing a real `BEGIN` before the first `SAVEPOINT` on a connection) correctly makes `db.begin_nested()` nest on SQLite by itself — confirmed with an isolated repro (4 sequential `begin_nested()` calls on one session accumulate correctly). The actual bug: three call sites in `agent/executor.py::_ingest_arc_sources` called `ensure_owned()` with **no `db` argument** inside the per-item ingest loop (before `retrieve()`, before the de-dupe queries, and at the top of the `for item in retrieved` loop). With `db=None`, `ClaimedTask.ensure_owned` → `task_queue.owns_task` opens its own short-lived `SessionLocal()`; under the `StaticPool` single shared SQLite connection used in tests/dev, that session's `with ... as own:` exit calls `own.close()`, which issues an implicit `ROLLBACK` on the **shared physical connection** — discarding every uncommitted `Source`/`SourceExcerpt` insert from earlier iterations of the main session's loop (reproduced directly: with the extra session interleaved, 4 ARC items collapsed to 1 row every time; without it, all 4 persisted). Fix: pass `db` on all three call sites (`ensure_owned(db)`), matching every other call site in the file and the function's own docstring ("With `db`, the check runs in that session's transaction"). `backend/tests/test_arc_fencing.py` → 6/6 passed (was 4 failed); full `pytest -q` run next. One bare `ensure_owned()` (no `db`) remains, immediately after the mid-run `db.commit()` before the LLM call — intentional per the adjacent comment (don't hold a lock across the model call) and not inside an open transaction, so not part of this bug — `P1` (was P3; this was breaking CI, not just a SQLite dev caveat) — found in the M0.9.1 review 2026-10-07

Any AI failure or undefined metric → `incomplete`, never `passed`. Runner `backend/evals/run_eval.py` (`python -m evals.run_eval screening` from `backend/`): real prompt + configured model over the gold set; per-paper errors recorded, not filled in; no model configured → stops; report (ids and outcomes only, no titles/abstracts) carries prompt ref, model id and a dataset hash of gold papers + criteria. **Regression rule** (`evals/baselines.py`, `tests/test_eval_baselines.py`): live prompt versions found by scanning `app/` for `load_prompt(*CONSTANT)` (any other call form fails the test); `evals/baselines.json` must have exactly one entry per live version; `eval: none` needs a reason; once the gold set has papers a screening entry must be `passed`/`failed` and point at a report for that prompt with the current dataset hash (`failed` needs `accepted_by` + reason). CI never calls a model; a **model change can't be detected by CI** — documented as a manual re-run. Not done: extraction runner (no AI extraction until M3.3), gap metrics (M3.9 + a gold format), and the screening criteria the gold set was labelled with (`evals/screening_criteria.json`, empty until Q10 — X.15's schema has no criteria field). Tests: `test_evaluation.py` + `test_eval_baselines.py` = 46 (mutation-checked: dropping the false-exclusion limit, failure→incomplete, report-hash and stale-entry checks each fails a test). `pytest -q` → 1696 passed, 6 skipped

Bulk is all-or-nothing, inclusive accept / exclusive discard threshold, never touches a suggestion without a confidence. Tests `test_suggestions_api.py` (17) + 9 role-matrix rows (`test_role_matrix.py` 725 passed with it). **Not built here:** the "never for this item" lock endpoint (X.46.3; the `locked` event kind and state exist).
    - Review 2026-10-12 (independent reviewer (AI subagent), `ReviewX462`): **ACCEPT with 1 minor finding.** `test_suggestions_api.py` 16 passed; `test_role_matrix.py -k suggestion` 63 passed. Checked by reading: WRITE/READ roles and 404 across projects; audit payloads hold ids, confidence and flags only (no suggestion text or guidance); `fence_guidance` wraps guidance with a random nonce; bulk is one transaction (all-or-nothing); undo and edit-accept only append events; gate targets refused at `create_suggestion`; double accept → 409 through the state check and the unique `(suggestion_id, seq)`. Not exercised: concurrent requests. Finding: bulk used `.limit(200)`, so with more than 200 matching suggestions the rest were silently left pending while the call reported success. **Fixed 2026-10-12 (claude):** bulk now covers every matching suggestion in its one transaction; regression test `test_bulk_covers_every_matching_suggestion_even_beyond_the_list_page_size` (203 suggestions) failed before the fix (200 of 203 accepted) and passes; `test_suggestions_api.py` + `test_suggestions.py` + `test_suggestion_guard.py` → 46 passed. Stays `[?]`; the fix has had no second reviewer.
    - Second review finding 2026-10-12 (independent reviewer, reported by the owner; confirmed by claude): `GET /suggestions` applied its 200-row limit **before** hiding discarded and superseded rows, so a project with many of those could have pending suggestions silently cut off. **Fixed 2026-10-12 (claude):** the list walks newest first, skips hidden rows (and, under dual-screen, AI screening suggestions) as it meets them, and stops at 200 shown rows. Regression test `test_the_list_limit_is_applied_after_hidden_suggestions_are_dropped` (limit patched to 3, five discarded newer rows, the pending one made the oldest with an explicit `created_at` because the column has one-second resolution; it failed before the fix with an empty list). `test_suggestions_api.py` + `test_suggestions.py` + `test_suggestion_guard.py` → 47 passed; role-matrix suggestion rows 63 passed. The fix has had no second reviewer. Note: the test sets `created_at` with a raw SQL `UPDATE`; that works because the guard covers events, not the suggestion row's timestamp.
  - [?] **X.46.3** "Never for this item": generic per-item AI lock (project, target kind/id), set/cleared by WRITE roles, audited; every AI producer (research runs, prescreen, synonyms, extraction) skips locked items server-side; `Note.ai_locked` enforced through the same check — `P1` — size S — deps: X.46.1, X.31.7 — owner: @claude — since: 2026-10-12 — started at the owner's instruction, after X.31.7 was built — built 2026-10-12, needs review (by someone other than @claude): `AiItemLock` model + migration `20261012_0047` (up/down/up + `alembic check` clean on scratch SQLite, file deleted), `app/ai_item_locks.py` (`is_locked`, `locked_ids`, `ensure_not_locked`, `set_lock`, `clear_lock`), `routers/ai_locks.py` (`GET /projects/{id}/ai-locks`, `PUT|DELETE /projects/{id}/ai-locks/{kind}/{id}`; WRITE roles, audited `ai_lock.set` / `ai_lock.cleared`; gates and notes refused with 422, unknown source/context item 404), 3 role-matrix rows. **Enforced server-side in:** research runs (locked sources and context items are left out before the prompt is built; `select_agent_context(locked_ids=)`, so the project page's "used by agent" flag agrees), pre-screen (`PrescreenResult.locked`), seed check (new `ai_locked` status and count, noted in the summary), and `suggestion_store.create_suggestion` (refuses a locked target, so any producer that stores a suggestion is covered, including the synonym and extraction producers X.46.5+ will add). Locking also moves the item's pending/discarded suggestions to the existing `locked` state. `Note.ai_locked` is read by the same `is_locked` check; a note is still locked on the note (`PATCH /api/notes/{id}/lock`), not through the project endpoint. **Design choices to confirm:** (1) target kind `screening` is the same item as `source` (a screening suggestion's target id is the source's id), so locking from a screening card locks the source for every AI step; (2) synonyms and extraction have no per-item target today (synonym requests carry an unsaved concept block; no AI extraction exists), so nothing there can be locked yet; (3) clearing a lock does not reopen suggestions already set to `locked`. Web: "Never for this item" (with a confirm) and "Allow AI for this item again" on `suggestion.js` cards. ## 3. Decisions log
| Date | Decision | By | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-10-01 | ARC runs as a separate HTTP microservice, opt-in per run, off by default | design doc | Keep ARC's heavy deps out of research-main; preserve the "no retrieval by default" boundary |
| 2026-10-01 | Merge the spec into this plan; spec milestones M0–M7 are the master structure; old Phase 1/0/2/3 tasks re-homed with `(was …)` annotations and statuses kept | user | Single source of truth |
| 2026-10-01 | Evolve the existing `research-main` app, not a greenfield rebuild | user | Auth, projects, hashed excerpts, durable runs, Claim tables, worker already exist |

| Q17 | Do you (or the university) hold an iThenticate/Turnitin licence with API access? (X.31.15) | claude (X.31 design) | open — owner will ask the library, date to follow |
| Q18 | Approve adding a pinned DOCX library (`python-docx`) to `backend/requirements.txt` for Word export? (X.31.13) | claude (X.31 design) | **answered** 2026-10-11: approved — pin the version and record its licence when X.31.13 is built |
| Q19 | AI-use statement wording: one generic template (recommended to start), or a specific journal's/university's policy text to match? (X.31.12) | claude (X.31 design) | **answered** 2026-10-11: one generic template for now; make the template file easy to replace with a specific journal/university text later |
| Q20 | Is server-side evidence enough, or should provenance reports be independently timestamped (RFC 3161, hash-only)? (X.31.12) | claude (X.31 design) | **answered** 2026-10-11: not now — server-side evidence only; revisit hash-only RFC 3161 timestamps if a journal or supervisor asks |
| Q21 | Version storage: keep every autosave forever (recommended) or thin after 30 days? Thinning needs a documented exception to "append-only". (X.31.8/X.31.9) | claude (X.31 design) | **answered** 2026-10-11: keep every autosave version forever, append-only; no thinning |
| ~~Q22~~ | "Data-driven stages": no such task exists in `plan.md`. Create one (stages configurable per discipline profile), or keep the fixed 19-stage `ProjectStage` enum? Only the X.31.2 stage chip is affected. | claude (X.31 design) | **answered** 2026-10-10 (approve a configurable "research type", with amendments; see Decisions and X.43) |
| Q23 | Retention & erasure policy: how long are projects, run snapshots, stored full texts and audit notes kept, and on an erasure request do we delete or pseudonymise the person (recommended: pseudonymise the user row, keep the audit trail under the research-integrity exemption for a fixed period)? Who signs this off (owner / university DPO)? (X.4.7, X.4.10) | claude (X.4 review) | open — **decision pending 2026-10-11**: owner will consult her university data-protection officer, date to follow. Recorded as **High**, a hard blocker for any real user and any participant data. Two options and their effects (not a legal recommendation): (a) pseudonymise the person, keep the audit trail under a research-integrity basis for a fixed period; (b) delete the person's data, which breaks the append-only audit trail's attribution for their rows |
| Q24 | Encryption at rest: confirm it is provided by the hosting (managed Postgres storage encryption, encrypted volumes or S3 SSE for files) rather than in the app — ties to Q13 hosting/region (X.4.6) | claude (X.4 review) | **partly answered** 2026-10-11: policy decided — encryption at rest is required from the host (managed Postgres storage encryption, encrypted volumes or S3 server-side encryption), added to the deployment checklist (X.4.6). **Still open:** confirm once hosting is chosen (Q13) |
| Q25 | Exit-condition levels: confirm each row's Required / Recommended / Information in `docs/stage-exit-conditions.md` (notably EC-4.2 conflicts = Required, EC-4.3 unscreened = Recommended, EC-5.4 every included study extracted = Recommended) (X.48) | claude (X.45) | **answered** 2026-10-11: approved, with the rule "Required only if it protects evidence, ethics or authorship; convenience/quality is Recommended or Information." Table re-checked against this rule: EC-4.7 (PRISMA numbers computed) downgraded Required→Recommended (a reporting-completeness condition, not an evidence/ethics/authorship protection). EC-0.4 (stage belongs to the project's chain) kept Required as a non-negotiable structural invariant — it has no "proceed with a reason" override at all, so it doesn't fit the Recommended/Information categories either; flagged here for the owner's awareness, not changed without her say |
| Q26 | "Never for this item": project-wide lock set by any owner/co-author (recommended), or per-person? (X.46.3) | claude (X.45) | **answered** 2026-10-11: the never-locked list (capture, drafting, exploring, reading later steps, going back) is project-wide for any role that may write — not a per-item toggle. Only the AI lock ("never send this to AI") is per item (X.46.3, unchanged) |
| Q27 | AI setting "Full" vs "Suggestions only": recommended meaning — Full = AI runs automatically on new items, Suggestions only = AI runs only when a person asks; both only ever suggest (X.31.7, ties to Q14) | claude (X.45) | **answered** 2026-10-11: Suggestions only = AI may comment, challenge, find evidence and check structure, but never supplies manuscript words. Full additionally allows reworded sentences the researcher may insert, always labelled in the writing record. Off = no text is sent to any AI |
| Q28 | Should G3/G4 approval refuse while that stage's Required conditions are unmet (recommended), or only stage advance? (X.48.3) | claude (X.45) | **answered** 2026-10-11: yes — approving G3 and G4 must itself refuse while any Required condition is unmet, not only stage advance, showing exactly which condition is missing with a one-click fix. Recommended conditions remain overridable with a short reason that is audited |

Single-reviewer mode (default) is untouched — gated on `project.dual_screen_enabled` throughout, proven by the full pre-existing `test_screening.py`/`test_role_matrix.py` suites passing unchanged.
